# Proposed OEIS comments: A224552 to A231941

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A224552

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A224552>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [2526](../papers/2501-3000/2526-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 3*a(n-2) + 2*a(n-3) - a(n-4) - a(n-5) + a(n-6) for n>7.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(50 - 80*x + 53*x^2 - 57*x^3 - 4*x^4 + 39*x^5 - 7*x^6) / ((1 - x)^2*(1 + x^2)*(1 - x - x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 31 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 36 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A224669

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A224669>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [8573](../papers/8501-9000/8573-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) -a(n-3).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 17 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [8471](../papers/8001-8500/8471-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) -5*a(n-2) +a(n-3) +2*a(n-4) -a(n-5) for n>6.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 17 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A225007

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A225007>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2558](../papers/2501-3000/2558-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 16 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A225008

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A225008>

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Sep 05 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A225034

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A225034>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [8979](../papers/8501-9000/8979-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) - (2*n+3)*a(n-1) - 3*(n-2)*a(n-2) = 0 for n>1. - _Bruno Berselli_, May 02 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 1, so the recurrence holds for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Conjecture: (-n+2)*a(n) +(-n+2)*a(n-1) +2*(3*n-11)*a(n-2) +2*(3*n-14)*a(n-3) +4*(-2*n+9)*a(n-4) +8*(-n+6)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 06 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A226751

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A226751>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [8348](../papers/8001-8500/8348-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 18*n*(2*n-1)*(55*n-76)*a(n) +(-11605*n^3+28521*n^2-20870*n+4536)*a(n-1) -24*(55*n-21)*(3*n-4)*(3*n-2)*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The entry posts no generating function, but its definition a(n) = [x^n] f(x)g(x)^n gives one: reading the extraction as a contour integral and summing the geometric series leaves a single integral whose only pole inside a small circle is the branch of x = t*g(x) through the origin, and the residue there is an algebraic function. The recurrence then follows from it, for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A227161

Proposed OEIS comment:


## A228278

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228278>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2769](../papers/2501-3000/2769-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228385

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228385>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8067](../papers/8001-8500/8067-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 6*a(n-2) - a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 - x)*(1 + x) / (1 - 2*x - 6*x^2 + x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 11 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 8 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228960

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228960>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [9109](../papers/9001-9500/9109-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 3*n*(1661*n-1820) *(3*n-1) *(3*n-2) *a(n) +(1927258*n^4 -17091925*n^3 +50171975*n^2 -59448794*n +24442440) *a(n-1) +12*(-705605*n^4 +6363374*n^3 -20228575*n^2 +27219817*n -13185456) *a(n-2) +54*(n-2) *(250934*n^3 -1892927*n^2 +4367836*n -3055963) *a(n-3) -486*(n-2)*(n-3) *(26090*n-34343) *(2*n-7) *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 15 2013.
> Conjecture 1: the supercongruence a(n*p^r) == a(n*p^(r-1)) (mod p^(2*r)) holds for positive integers n and r and all primes p >= 5. Cf. A350383.
> Conjecture 2: let k be a positive integer, m an integer and let f(x) = g(x)/h(x), where both g(x) and h(x) are finite products of cyclotomic polynomials. Then the same supercongruences hold, except for a finite number of primes p depending on f(x), for the sequence {a_(k,m,f)(n): n >= 0} defined by a_(k,m,f)(n) = [x^(k*n)] f(x)^(m*n). (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*a(n-1) - 29*a(n-2) + 20*a(n-3) - 4*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 - 2*x^2 + 3*x^3) / (1 - 5*x + 2*x^2)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Sep 17 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 72 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

```

Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
